\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{\sqrt{2\pi}},&\text{if }\tau^{2}>1.\end{cases}

Combining the two inequalities in the display above yields

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\;\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{TV}}\big(\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}_{\mathsf{plugin}}(X_{\theta}),Y_{\theta}\big)\geq\frac{(\sqrt{2}-1)e^{-3/2}}{2\pi}\cdot\min\{1,\tau^{-2}\}\quad\text{for all }\tau\in\real.

Clearly, this implies that one must take
\tau^{2}\gtrsim\epsilon^{-1}
to guarantee
\epsilon
-TV deficiency.
∎

Appendix B
Proofs of supporting lemmas

In this section, we provide additional proofs deferred from the main text.

B.1
Proof of Lemma
2

By definition, we have

f^{(n)}(x)=\sum_{k=1}^{K}c_{k}f_{k}^{(n)}(x)\quad\text{for all }n\in\mathbb{N}\text{ and }x\in\real.

Consequently, applying the triangle inequality yields that for all
t\in\real
,

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{|f^{(j)}(t)|}{\big(\widetilde{C}_{f}\big)^{j}j!}\bigg)^{n}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{K}|c_{k}|\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{|f_{k}^{(j)}(t)|}{\big(\widetilde{C}_{f}\big)^{j}j!}\bigg)^{n}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}|c_{\ell}|\bigg)^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bigg(\frac{|c_{k}|}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}|c_{\ell}|}\bigg)^{n}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{|f_{k}^{(j)}(t)|}{\big(\widetilde{C}_{f}\big)^{j}j!}\bigg)^{n}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bigg(\frac{|c_{k}|}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}|c_{\ell}|}\bigg)^{n}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{|f_{k}^{(j)}(t)|}{\big(\widetilde{C}_{f_{k}}\big)^{j}j!}\bigg)^{n},

where in the last step we use
\widetilde{C}_{f}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}|c_{\ell}|\cdot\max_{k\in[K]}\{\widetilde{C}_{f_{k}}\}
. By letting
t(x)=(\theta+\sqrt{2}\sigma x)/\tau
and integrating over
x\in\real
yields

\displaystyle\int\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{|f^{(j)}(t(x))|}{\big(\widetilde{C}_{f}\big)^{j}j!}\bigg)^{n}\frac{e^{-x^{2}}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bigg(\frac{|c_{k}|}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}|c_{\ell}|}\bigg)^{n}\int\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{|f_{k}^{(j)}(t)|}{\big(\widetilde{C}_{f_{k}}\big)^{j}j!}\bigg)^{n}\frac{e^{-x^{2}}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\leq}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bigg(\frac{|c_{k}|}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}|c_{\ell}|}\bigg)^{n}\cdot(C_{f_{k}}n)!

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\max_{k\in[K]}\{C_{f_{k}}\}\cdot n\Big)!,

where in step
{\sf(i)}
we use the fact that
f_{k}
satisfies Assumption
3
with parameters
\widetilde{C}_{f_{k}}
and
C_{f_{k}}
. This verifies that
f
satisfies Assumption
3
. We next bound
L_{f}(n)
and
\widetilde{L}_{f}(n,\tau)
. By definition (
35
), we obtain

\displaystyle L_{f}(n)=\sup_{|x|\leq 1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{|f^{(j)}(x)|}{\big(\widetilde{C}_{f}\big)^{j}j!}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}|c_{k}|\sup_{|x|\leq 1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{|f_{k}^{(j)}(x)|}{\big(\widetilde{C}_{f}\big)^{j}j!}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\leq}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{|c_{k}|}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}|c_{\ell}|}\sup_{|x|\leq 1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{|f_{k}^{(j)}(x)|}{\big(\widetilde{C}_{f_{k}}\big)^{j}j!}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{|c_{k}|}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}|c_{\ell}|}L_{f_{k}}(n),

where in step
{\sf(i)}
we use
\widetilde{C}_{f}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}|c_{\ell}|\cdot\max_{k\in[K]}\{\widetilde{C}_{f_{k}}\}
. Following identical steps yields the desired bound for
\widetilde{L}_{f}(n,\tau)
.
∎

B.2
Proof of Lemma
4

We prove each part separately.

Proof of part (i).
Applying Faà di Bruno’s formula as in Eq. (
6.1.1
) yields that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and all
x,y\in\real
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}^{(n)}v(y;x)=\frac{1}{\tau}e^{-\psi(\frac{y-x}{\tau})}\cdot n!\cdot\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{n}}\frac{(-1)^{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}}{m_{1}!\,m_{2}!\cdots m_{n}!}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\bigg(\frac{\psi^{(j)}(\frac{y-x}{\tau})}{(-\tau)^{j}j!}\bigg)^{m_{j}},

(140)

where the sum is over all
n
-tuples of nonnegative integers
(m_{1},\dots,m_{n})
satisfying
\sum_{j=1}^{n}jm_{j}=n
. Consequently, using definition (
14
) yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{N}^{\star}(y\mid x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau}e^{-\psi(\frac{y-x}{\tau})}\Bigg(1+\sum_{k=1}^{N}\frac{(-1)^{k}\sigma^{2k}(2k)!}{(2k)!!}\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{2k}}\frac{(-1)^{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{2k}}}{m_{1}!\,m_{2}!\cdots m_{2k}!}\prod_{j=1}^{2k}\bigg(\frac{\psi^{(j)}(\frac{y-x}{\tau})}{(-\tau)^{j}j!}\bigg)^{m_{j}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\geq}\frac{1}{\tau}e^{-\psi(\frac{y-x}{\tau})}\Bigg(1-\sum_{k=1}^{N}\frac{\sigma^{2k}(2k)!}{(2k)!!}\frac{\lambda^{2k}}{\tau^{2k}}\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{2k}}\frac{1}{m_{1}!\,m_{2}!\cdots m_{2k}!}\prod_{j=1}^{2k}\bigg(\frac{\big|\psi^{(j)}(\frac{y-x}{\tau})\big|}{\lambda^{j}j!}\bigg)^{m_{j}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(ii)}}{\geq}\frac{1}{\tau}e^{-\psi(\frac{y-x}{\tau})}\Bigg(1-\sum_{k=1}^{N}\frac{\sigma^{2k}(2k)!}{(2k)!!}\frac{\lambda^{2k}}{\tau^{2k}}\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{2k}}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{2k}\frac{\big|\psi^{(j)}(\frac{y-x}{\tau})\big|}{\lambda^{j}j!}\bigg)^{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{2k}}\Bigg),

where in step
{\sf(i)}
we apply the triangle inequality and use
\sum_{j=1}^{2k}jm_{j}=2k
so that

\frac{\lambda^{2k}}{\tau^{2k}}\prod_{j=1}^{2k}\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda^{j}}\bigg)^{m_{j}}=\prod_{j=1}^{2k}\bigg(\frac{1}{\tau^{j}}\bigg)^{m_{j}},

and in step
{\sf(ii)}
we use the multinomial theorem. Continuing, note that we have
(y-x)/\tau\in\mathcal{A}(\psi,2N,\lambda)
(
24
) by assumption, and so we obtain

\sum_{j=1}^{2k}\frac{\big|\psi^{(j)}(\frac{y-x}{\tau})\big|}{\lambda^{j}j!}\leq 1\quad\text{for all }k\in[N].

Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{N}^{\star}(y\mid x)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\tau}e^{-\psi(\frac{y-x}{\tau})}\bigg(1-\sum_{k=1}^{N}\frac{\sigma^{2k}(2k)!}{(2k)!!}\frac{\lambda^{2k}}{\tau^{2k}}\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{2k}}1\bigg)

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\geq}\frac{1}{\tau}e^{-\psi(\frac{y-x}{\tau})}\bigg(1-\sum_{k=1}^{N}\frac{\sigma^{2k}(2k)^{k}\lambda^{2k}e^{2k}}{\tau^{2k}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(ii)}}{\geq}\frac{1}{\tau}e^{-\psi(\frac{y-x}{\tau})}\bigg(1-\sum_{k=1}^{N}4^{-k}\bigg)\geq 0.

Here in step
{\sf(i)}
, we use the fact there are at most
e^{2k}
terms in the summation and that
(2k)!/(2k)!!\leq(2k)^{k}
. On the other hand, step
{\sf(ii)}
holds under the assumption that
\tau\geq 8e\lambda\sigma N
, which is the condition in the lemma statement. This proves part (i) of Lemma
4
.

Proof of part (ii).
We first verify the first part of Eq. (
83
). Note that
\rho(t)=g(\psi(t))
, where
g(t)=e^{-t}
. Applying Faà di Bruno’s formula yields

\displaystyle\rho^{(n)}(t)=\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{n}}\frac{n!}{m_{1}!\,m_{2}!\cdots m_{n}!}(-1)^{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}e^{-\psi(t)}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\bigg(\frac{\psi^{(j)}(t)}{j!}\bigg)^{m_{j}}.

Applying the triangle inequality yields

\displaystyle\big|\rho^{(n)}(t)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{n}}\frac{n!}{m_{1}!\,m_{2}!\cdots m_{n}!}e^{-\psi(t)}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\bigg(\frac{|\psi^{(j)}(t)|}{j!}\bigg)^{m_{j}}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\leq}n!\cdot\lambda^{n}\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{n}}\frac{e^{-\psi(t)}}{m_{1}!\,m_{2}!\cdots m_{n}!}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\bigg(\frac{|\psi^{(j)}(t)|}{\lambda^{j}j!}\bigg)^{m_{j}}

\displaystyle\leq n!\cdot\lambda^{n}\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{n}}e^{-\psi(t)}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{|\psi^{(j)}(t)|}{\lambda^{j}j!}\bigg)^{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}},

(141)

where in step
{\sf(i)}
we use
\sum_{j=1}^{n}jm_{j}=n
so that
\prod_{j=1}^{n}(\frac{1}{\lambda^{j}})^{m_{j}}=\lambda^{-n}
and in step
{\sf(ii)}
we use the multinomial theorem. By setting
\lambda=\widetilde{C}_{\psi}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int\big|\rho^{(n)}(t)\big|\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq n!\cdot\big(\widetilde{C}_{\psi}\big)^{n}\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{n}}\int e^{-\psi(t)}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{|\psi^{(j)}(t)|}{\big(\widetilde{C}_{\psi}\big)^{j}j!}\bigg)^{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\leq}n!\cdot\big(\widetilde{C}_{\psi}\big)^{n}\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{n}}(C_{\psi}n)!\overset{{\sf(ii)}}{\leq}n!\cdot\big(\widetilde{C}_{\psi}\big)^{n}e^{n}\cdot(C_{\psi}n)!<+\infty,

where in step
{\sf(i)}
we use Assumption
2
and in step
{\sf(ii)}
we use the fact that there are at most
e^{n}
terms in the summation. Since
|\rho^{(n)}(t)|
is continuous and nonnegative, we must have
\lim_{t\uparrow+\infty}\rho^{(n)}(t)=\lim_{t\downarrow-\infty}\rho^{(n)}(t)=0
. Consequently, we obtain for
n\geq 1
that

\int\rho^{(n)}(t)\mathrm{d}t=\int\mathrm{d}\rho^{(n-1)}(t)=\Big[\rho^{(n-1)}(t)\Big]\Big|_{-\infty}^{+\infty}=0.

This proves the first part of Eq. (
83
).

We now turn to prove the second part of Eq. (
83
). Using Eq. (
140
) and applying triangle inequality, we obtain for all
n\geq 1
that

\displaystyle\big|\nabla_{x}^{(n)}v(y;x)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\tau}e^{-\psi(\frac{y-x}{\tau})}\cdot n!\cdot\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{n}}\frac{1}{m_{1}!\,m_{2}!\cdots m_{n}!}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\bigg(\frac{|\psi^{(j)}(\frac{y-x}{\tau})|}{\tau^{j}j!}\bigg)^{m_{j}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\tau}e^{-\psi(\frac{y-x}{\tau})}\cdot n!\cdot\frac{\lambda^{n}}{\tau^{n}}\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{n}}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{|\psi^{(j)}(\frac{y-x}{\tau})|}{\lambda^{j}j!}\bigg)^{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}},

(142)

where in the last step we use
(\lambda/\tau)^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{n}(\frac{1}{\lambda^{j}})^{m_{j}}=\prod_{j=1}^{n}(\frac{1}{\tau^{j}})^{m_{j}}
and then apply the multinomial theorem. Integrating over
y
yields

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{y-x}{\tau}\in\mathcal{A}^{\complement}(\psi,2N,\lambda)}\big|\nabla_{x}^{(n)}v(y;x)\big|\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq n!\cdot\frac{\lambda^{n}}{\tau^{n}}\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{n}}\int_{\frac{y-x}{\tau}\in\mathcal{A}^{\complement}(\psi,2N,\lambda)}\frac{1}{\tau}e^{-\psi(\frac{y-x}{\tau})}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{|\psi^{(j)}(\frac{y-x}{\tau})|}{\lambda^{j}j!}\bigg)^{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\leq}n!\cdot\frac{\lambda^{n}}{\tau^{n}}\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{n}}\int_{\mathcal{A}^{\complement}(\psi,2N,\lambda)}e^{-\psi(t)}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{|\psi^{(j)}(t)|}{\lambda^{j}j!}\bigg)^{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq n!\cdot\frac{\lambda^{n}}{\tau^{n}}\cdot e^{n}\cdot\mathsf{Err}(\psi,2N,\lambda),

where in step
{\sf(i)}
we use the change of variables
t=(y-x)/\tau
. This proves Eq. (
83
).
∎

B.3
Proof of Lemma
5

Note that by definition (
33
) and Eq. (
99
), we have
v(y;x)=\phi(g(x))
. Applying Faà di Bruno’s formula yields

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}^{(n)}v(y;x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{n}}\frac{n!}{m_{1}!\,m_{2}!\cdots m_{n}!}\cdot\phi^{(m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n})}(g(x))\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{n}\bigg(\frac{g^{(j)}(x)}{j!}\bigg)^{m_{j}}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{=}n!\cdot\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{n}}\frac{(-1)^{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}}{m_{1}!\,m_{2}!\cdots m_{n}!}\cdot\phi(g(x))\cdot H_{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}(g(x))\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{n}\bigg(\frac{f^{(j)}(x/\tau)}{2\tau^{j}j!}\bigg)^{m_{j}}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(ii)}}{=}\frac{(\widetilde{C}_{f})^{n}}{\tau^{n}}\cdot n!\cdot\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{n}}\frac{(-1)^{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}}{m_{1}!\,m_{2}!\cdots m_{n}!}\cdot\phi(g(x))\cdot H_{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}(g(x))\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{n}\bigg(\frac{f^{(j)}(x/\tau)}{2(\widetilde{C}_{f})^{j}j!}\bigg)^{m_{j}},

where in all steps the summation is over all nonnegative integers
(m_{1},\cdots,m_{n})
satisfying
\sum_{j=1}^{n}jm_{j}=n
. In step
{\sf(i)}
, we use the definition of Hermite polynomials (
6
) and in step
{\sf(ii)}
, we use
\prod_{j=1}^{n}(\frac{1}{\tau^{j}})^{m_{j}}=\frac{(\widetilde{C}_{f})^{n}}{\tau^{n}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}(\frac{1}{(\widetilde{C}_{f})^{j}})^{m_{j}}
.
Using the definition of
h_{j}(x)
in Eq. (
99
) and combining the pieces then yields Eq. (
100
).

To prove the second claim of the lemma, note that from Ineq. (
62a
), we have

\displaystyle\big|\phi(g(x))\cdot H_{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}(g(x))\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{-g(x)^{2}/2}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\pi^{1/4}\cdot(m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n})!\cdot 2^{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-g(x)^{2}/2}\cdot 2^{n}\cdot(m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n})!.

Now applying the triangle inequality to the display before yields

\displaystyle\big|\nabla_{x}^{(n)}v(y;x)\big|

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{g(x)^{2}}{2}}\cdot\frac{(2\widetilde{C}_{f})^{n}}{\tau^{n}}\cdot n!\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{n}}\frac{(m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n})!}{m_{1}!\,m_{2}!\cdots m_{n}!}\prod_{j=1}^{n}|h_{j}(x)|^{m_{j}}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\leq}e^{-\frac{g(x)^{2}}{2}}\cdot\frac{(2\widetilde{C}_{f})^{n}}{\tau^{n}}\cdot n!\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{n}}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}|h_{j}(x)|\bigg)^{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{g(x)^{2}}{2}}\cdot\frac{(2\widetilde{C}_{f})^{n}}{\tau^{n}}\cdot n!\cdot e^{n}\cdot\bigg[\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}|h_{j}(x)|\bigg)^{n}\vee 1\bigg]

where in step
{\sf(i)}
we use the multinomial theorem so that

\frac{(m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n})!}{m_{1}!\,m_{2}!\cdots m_{n}!}\prod_{j=1}^{n}|h_{j}(x)|^{m_{j}}\leq\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}|h_{j}(x)|\bigg)^{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}},

and in the last step we use there are at most
e^{n}
in the summation and
m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}\leq n
.
This concludes the proof. ∎

B.4
Proof of Lemma
7

We prove each part separately. Recall the shorthand
h_{j}(x)
,
g(x)
, and
\phi(x)
from Eq. (
99
).

Proof of Lemma
7
(a).

From Eq. (
62c
), we obtain for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
n\geq 1
, and
x\in\real
that

|H_{n}(x)|\leq n!\cdot\frac{n}{2}\cdot(1\vee 2|x|)^{n}.

Consequently, if
|g(x)|=|f(x/\tau)-y|/2\leq\frac{\sqrt{\tau}}{2}
, then for
\tau\geq 4
and
1\leq m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}\leq n
, we have

\displaystyle\big|H_{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}(g(x))\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}{2}\cdot(m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n})!\cdot\tau^{\frac{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n}{2}\cdot(m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n})!\cdot\tau^{\frac{n}{2}}.

Using the bound in the above display along with Eq. (
100
), we obtain for
n\geq 1
that

\displaystyle\big|\nabla_{x}^{(n)}v(y;x)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\phi(g(x))\cdot\frac{n}{2}\cdot\tau^{\frac{n}{2}}\cdot n!\cdot\frac{(\widetilde{C}_{f})^{n}}{\tau^{n}}\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{n}}\frac{(m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n})!}{m_{1}!\,m_{2}!\cdots m_{n}!}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{n}\big|h_{j}(x)\big|^{m_{j}}

\displaystyle\leq\phi(g(x))\cdot\frac{n}{2}\cdot\tau^{\frac{n}{2}}\cdot n!\cdot\frac{(\widetilde{C}_{f})^{n}}{\tau^{n}}\cdot e^{n}\cdot\bigg[\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}|h_{j}(x)|\bigg)^{n}\vee 1\bigg],

(143)

where in the last step we use the multinomial theorem so that

\frac{(m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n})!}{m_{1}!\,m_{2}!\cdots m_{n}!}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{n}\big|h_{j}(x)\big|^{m_{j}}\leq\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}|h_{j}(x)|\bigg)^{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}\leq\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}|h_{j}(x)|\bigg)^{n}\vee 1,

and we also use there are at most
e^{n}
terms in the summation (see Ineq. (
69
)). Continuing, by using the signed kernel definition (
14
) for
\sigma=1
, we obtain for
|x|\leq\tau
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{N}^{\star}(y\mid x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{N}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{(2k)!!}\nabla_{x}^{(2k)}v(y;x)

\displaystyle\geq v(y;x)-\sum_{k=1}^{N}\frac{1}{(2k)!!}\big|\nabla_{x}^{(2k)}v(y;x)\big|

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\geq}\phi(g(x))-\sum_{k=1}^{N}\frac{1}{(2k)!!}\cdot\phi(g(x))\cdot\frac{2k}{2}\cdot(2k)!\cdot\tau^{k}\cdot\bigg(\frac{\widetilde{C}_{f}}{\tau}\bigg)^{2k}e^{2k}\bigg[\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{2k}|h_{j}(x)|\bigg)\vee 1\bigg]^{2k}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(ii)}}{\geq}\phi(g(x))\cdot\Bigg(1-\sum_{k=1}^{N}\frac{\Big(e^{3}\big(\widetilde{C}_{f}\big)^{2}\big(L_{f}(2k)\vee 1\big)2k\Big)^{k}}{\tau^{k}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(iii)}}{\geq}\phi(g(x))\cdot\Bigg(1-\sum_{k=1}^{N}\frac{1}{2^{k}}\Bigg)\geq 0.

In step
{\sf(i)}
above, we use Ineq. (
B.4
) for
n=2k
. In step
{\sf(ii)}
, we use the inequalities
k\leq e^{k}
and
(2k)!/(2k)!!=(2k-1)!!\leq(2k)^{k}
, and also that

\sum_{j=1}^{2k}|h_{j}(x)|\leq L_{f}(2k)\quad\text{for all }|x|\leq\tau,

which in turn follows from combining Eq. (
35
) and Eq. (
99
).
Step
{\sf(iii)}
holds if

\tau\geq 2e^{3}\big(\widetilde{C}_{f}\big)^{2}\big(L_{f}(2N)\vee 1\big)2N\geq 2e^{3}\big(\widetilde{C}_{f}\big)^{2}\big(L_{f}(2k)\vee 1\big)2k\quad\text{for all }k\in[N],

as assumed in the lemma.
This concludes the proof of part (a).

Proof of Lemma
7
(b)

Using Ineq. (
101
) and noting that
g(x)=(f(x/\tau)-y)/2
(Eq. (
99
)), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{f(\frac{x}{\tau})+\sqrt{\tau}}^{+\infty}\big|\nabla_{x}^{(2k)}v(y;x)\big|\mathrm{d}y+\int_{-\infty}^{f(\frac{x}{\tau})-\sqrt{\tau}}\big|\nabla_{x}^{(2k)}v(y;x)\big|\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq(2k)!\cdot e^{2k}\cdot\bigg(\frac{2\widetilde{C}_{f}}{\tau}\bigg)^{2k}\cdot\bigg[\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{2k}\big|h_{j}(x)\big|\bigg)^{2k}\vee 1\bigg]\cdot 2\int_{\sqrt{\tau}}^{+\infty}e^{-t^{2}/8}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\leq}2\sqrt{2\pi}\exp(-\tau/8)\cdot(2k)!\cdot\bigg(\frac{2e\widetilde{C}_{f}}{\tau}\bigg)^{2k}\cdot\big(L_{f}(2k)\vee 1\big)^{2k},

where in step
{\sf(i)}
we use the fact that
|x|\leq\tau
as well as the definitions of
h_{j}(x)
(
99
) and
L_{f}(n)
(
35
). In particular, these yield

\sum_{j=1}^{2k}\big|h_{j}(x)\big|\leq L_{f}(2k)\quad\text{and}\quad 2\int_{\sqrt{\tau}}^{+\infty}e^{-t^{2}/8}\mathrm{d}t\leq 2\sqrt{2\pi}\exp(-\tau/8).

Proof of Lemma
7
(c)

Using the closed-form expression for
\nabla_{x}^{(n)}v(y;x)
(Eq. (
100
)) and noting that
g(x)=(f(x/\tau)-y)/2
(Eq. (
99
)), we obtain

\displaystyle\int\nabla_{x}^{(n)}v(y;x)\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{n}}\frac{n!\cdot(-1)^{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}}{m_{1}!\,m_{2}!\cdots m_{n}!}\bigg(\frac{\widetilde{C}_{f}}{\tau}\bigg)^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\Big(h_{j}(x)/2\Big)^{m_{j}}2\int H_{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}(t)\cdot\phi(t)\mathrm{d}t=0.

In the last step, we use the facts that
m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}\geq 1
and
H_{0}(x)=1
along with the orthogonal property of Hermite polynomials. In particular, these imply that

\displaystyle\int H_{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}(t)\cdot\phi(t)\mathrm{d}t=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\big\langle H_{m_{1}+\cdots+m_{n}}(\cdot),H_{0}(\cdot)\big\rangle=0.

This concludes the proof of the final part of the lemma.
∎

B.5
Proof of Lemma
8

Note that there are two differences between the tensor
\widetilde{T}
in Eq. (
133
) and the tensor
T
in Eq. (
43
): (i) the additive Gaussian noise
\widetilde{\zeta}
is asymmetric, and (ii) the spike
\widetilde{v}
is discrete. We now construct two procedures
\mathcal{R}_{1},\mathcal{R}_{2}:\mathbb{R}^{n^{\otimes s}}\to\mathbb{R}^{n^{\otimes s}}
, each designed to resolve one of these two differences. Our overall reduction is the composition of the two, i.e.,

\mathcal{R}\big(\widetilde{T}\big):=\mathcal{R}_{2}\circ\mathcal{R}_{1}\big(\widetilde{T}\big).

Construction of reduction
\mathcal{R}_{1}
to make instance symmetric.

We let

\mathcal{R}_{1}\big(\widetilde{T}\big)=\frac{1}{s!}\sum_{\pi\in\in\mathfrak{S}_{s}}\big(\widetilde{T}\big)^{\pi},

where
\big(\widetilde{T}\big)^{\pi}\in\real^{n^{\otimes s}}
denotes the permutation of the tensor
\widetilde{T}
so that

\big(\widetilde{T}\big)^{\pi}_{i_{1},\dots,i_{s}}=\big(\widetilde{T}\big)^{\pi}_{i_{\pi(1)},\dots,i_{\pi(s)}}\quad\text{for all indices}\quad i_{1},\cdots,i_{s}\in[n].

Consequently, by the definition of
\widetilde{T}
in Eq. (
133
), we obtain

\mathcal{R}_{1}\big(\widetilde{T}\big)=\beta(\sqrt{n}\widetilde{v})^{\otimes s}+\zeta,\quad where\quad\widetilde{v}\sim\mathsf{Unif}\big(\big\{\tfrac{-1}{\sqrt{n}},\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\big\}^{n}\big)

and the noise tensor
\zeta
is now symmetric and satisfies

\zeta\overset{(d)}{=}\frac{1}{s!}\sum_{\pi\in\in\mathfrak{S}_{s}}G^{\pi},

where
G\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{\otimes s}}
is an asymmetric tensor with entries that are
\mathsf{i.i.d.}

\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, and
G^{\pi}\in\real^{n^{\otimes s}}
satisfies
G^{\pi}_{i_{1},\dots,i_{s}}=G_{i_{\pi(1)},\dots,i_{\pi(s)}}
for all indices
i_{1},\cdots,i_{s}\in[n]
.

Construction of reduction
\mathcal{R}_{2}
to spread the signal spike.

We next construct the second procedure
\mathcal{R}_{2}
, which transforms the tensor
\mathcal{R}_{1}\big(\widetilde{T}\big)
into one whose spike
v
is uniformly distributed on the unit sphere and whose additive Gaussian noise remains independent of
v
and symmetric Gaussian. For notational convenience, we let

\overline{T}:=\mathcal{R}_{1}\big(\widetilde{T}\big)=\beta(\sqrt{n}\widetilde{v})^{\otimes s}+\zeta.

We first review some definitions. For a tensor
T\in\real^{n\otimes s}
, define its vectorization as

\mathsf{vec}(T)=\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\cdots\sum_{i_{s}=1}^{n}T_{i_{1},\dots,i_{s}}\times e_{i_{1}}\otimes e_{i_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes e_{i_{s}},

where
e_{i}
is
i
-th standard basis vector in
n
and
\otimes
denotes the Kronecker product. For any permutation
\pi:[s]\to[s]
, define
P_{\pi}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{s}\times n^{s}}
by its action on the Kronecker basis:

P_{\pi}\big(e_{i_{1}}\otimes e_{i_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes e_{i_{s}}\big)\;=\;e_{i_{\pi^{-1}(1)}}\otimes e_{i_{\pi^{-1}(2)}}\otimes\cdots\otimes e_{i_{\pi^{-1}(s)}},\quad i_{1},\dots,i_{s}\in[n].

Since
P_{\pi}
maps the standard basis of
\mathbb{R}^{n^{s}}
bijectively to itself, it is a permutation matrix.

We now construct the reduction
\mathcal{R}_{2}:\real^{n\otimes s}\rightarrow\real^{n\otimes s}
: Let
U\in\real^{n\times n}
be a unitary matrix that is drawn uniformly at random from the set
\{U\in\real^{n\times n}:UU^{\top}=I_{n}\}
. Given
\overline{T}\in\real^{n^{\otimes s}}
, construct
\mathcal{R}_{2}\big(\overline{T}\big)
such that

\mathsf{vec}\big(\mathcal{R}_{2}\big(\overline{T}\big)\big)=(U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U)\;\mathsf{vec}\big(\overline{T}\big).

The tensor
\mathcal{R}_{2}\big(\overline{T}\big)
by suitably stacking this vector into the appropriate tensor dimension.

The reduction achieves zero-deficiency.

To verify
\mathcal{R}_{2}\big(\overline{T}\big)\overset{(d)}{=}T
, it is equivalent to verify
\mathsf{vec}\big(\mathcal{R}_{2}\big(\overline{T}\big)\big)\overset{(d)}{=}\mathsf{vec}(T)
. Note that we have by definition,

\displaystyle\mathsf{vec}\big(\mathcal{R}_{2}\big(\overline{T}\big)\big)

\displaystyle=(U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U)\;\mathsf{vec}\big(\overline{T}\big)

\displaystyle=(U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U)\;\Big(\beta\times(\sqrt{n}\widetilde{v})\otimes(\sqrt{n}\widetilde{v})\cdots\otimes(\sqrt{n}\widetilde{v})+\mathsf{vec}\big(\zeta\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\beta\times(\sqrt{n}U\widetilde{v})\otimes(\sqrt{n}U\widetilde{v})\otimes\cdots\otimes(\sqrt{n}U\widetilde{v})+(U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U)\;\mathsf{vec}\big(\zeta\big).

We now claim two equalities, whose proofs we defer to the end:

\displaystyle\mathsf{vec}\big(\zeta\big)=\frac{1}{s!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathfrak{S}_{s}}P_{\pi}\;\mathsf{vec}\big(G\big)\quad\text{and}

(144a)

\displaystyle(U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U)\;\mathsf{vec}\big(\zeta\big)=\frac{1}{s!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathfrak{S}_{s}}P_{\pi}\;(U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U)\;\mathsf{vec}\big(G\big).

(144b)

Putting the pieces together yields

\displaystyle\mathsf{vec}\big(\mathcal{R}_{2}\big(\overline{T}\big)\big)=\beta\times(\sqrt{n}U\widetilde{v})\otimes(\sqrt{n}U\widetilde{v})\otimes\cdots\otimes(\sqrt{n}U\widetilde{v})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{s!}}\sum_{\pi\in\mathfrak{S}_{s}}P_{\pi}\;(U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U)\;\mathsf{vec}\big(G\big),

(145)

and by definition of
T
(
43
) and Eq. (
144a
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{vec}\big(T\big)=\beta\times(\sqrt{n}v)\otimes(\sqrt{n}v)\otimes\cdots\otimes(\sqrt{n}v)+\frac{1}{s!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathfrak{S}_{s}}P_{\pi}\;\mathsf{vec}\big(G\big).

(146)

We now compare Eq. (
145
) and Eq. (
146
) to verify that
\mathsf{vec}\big(\mathcal{R}\big(\overline{T}\big)\big)\overset{(d)}{=}\mathsf{vec}(T)
. First, note that
\widetilde{v}\sim\mathsf{Unif}\big(\big\{\tfrac{-1}{\sqrt{n}},\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\big\}^{n}\big)
,
U
is a unitary matrix that is selected uniformly at random and is independent of
\widetilde{v}
, and
v\sim\mathsf{Unif}\big(\mathbb{S}^{n-1}\big)
. We have
U\widetilde{v}\overset{(d)}{=}v
. So the first terms in the RHS of Eq. (
145
) and Eq. (
146
) are equally distributed. Second, note that

\mathsf{vec}\big(G\big)\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{n^{s}})
and we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[(U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U)\;\mathsf{vec}\big(G\big)\big]=0\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big((U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U)\;\mathsf{vec}\big(G\big)\big)^{\top}\big((U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U)\;\mathsf{vec}\big(G\big)\big)\Big]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big(\mathsf{vec}\big(G\big)\big)^{\top}\big(U^{\top}\otimes U^{\top}\otimes\cdots\otimes U^{\top}\big)\big(U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U\big)\;\mathsf{vec}\big(G\big)\Big]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big(\mathsf{vec}\big(G\big)\big)^{\top}\big((U^{\top}U)\otimes(U^{\top}U)\otimes\cdots\otimes(U^{\top}U)\big)\;\mathsf{vec}\big(G\big)\Big]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big(\mathsf{vec}\big(G\big)\big)^{\top}\;\mathsf{vec}\big(G\big)\Big]=I_{n^{s}}.

Consequently, we obtain

(U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U)\;\mathsf{vec}\big(G\big)\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{n^{s}}).

This proves that the second terms in the RHS of Eq. (
145
) and Eq. (
146
) are equally distributed. Therefore, we have verified
\mathsf{vec}\big(\mathcal{R}\big(\overline{T}\big)\big)\overset{(d)}{=}\mathsf{vec}(T)
.
It remains to prove the claimed equalities (
144
).

Proof of Eq. (
144a
)

Note that we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{vec}\big(\zeta\big)=\frac{1}{s!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathfrak{S}_{s}}\mathsf{vec}\big(G^{\pi}\big).

By by definition, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{vec}\big(G^{\pi}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\cdots\sum_{i_{s}=1}^{n}G_{i_{\pi(1)},\dots,i_{\pi(s)}}\;e_{i_{1}}\otimes e_{i_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes e_{i_{s}}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{=}\sum_{i_{\pi(1)}=1}^{n}\cdots\sum_{i_{\pi(s)}=1}^{n}G_{i_{\pi(1)},\dots,i_{\pi(s)}}\;e_{i_{1}}\otimes e_{i_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes e_{i_{s}}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(ii)}}{=}\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{n}\cdots\sum_{j_{s}=1}^{n}G_{j_{1},\dots,j_{s}}\;e_{j_{\pi^{-1}(1)}}\otimes e_{j_{\pi^{-1}(2)}}\otimes\cdots\otimes e_{j_{\pi^{-1}(s)}}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(iii)}}{=}\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{n}\cdots\sum_{j_{s}=1}^{n}G_{j_{1},\dots,j_{s}}\;P_{\pi}\Big(e_{j_{1}}\otimes e_{j_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes e_{j_{s}}\Big)

\displaystyle=P_{\pi}\;\mathsf{vec}\big(G\big),

where in step
{\sf(i)}
we reorder the summation sequence, in step
{\sf(ii)}
we rename the indices by letting

j_{1}=i_{\pi(1)},\quad j_{2}=i_{\pi(2)},\quad\cdots,\quad j_{s}=i_{\pi(s)}

so that

i_{1}=j_{\pi^{-1}(1)},\quad i_{2}=j_{\pi^{-1}(2)},\quad\cdots,\quad i_{s}=j_{\pi^{-1}(s)},

and in step
{\sf(iii)}
we use the definition of
P_{\pi}
. Summing over permutations completes the proof.

Proof of Eq. (
144b
)

We have

\displaystyle(U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U)\;\mathsf{vec}\big(\zeta\big)=\frac{1}{s!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathfrak{S}_{s}}(U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U)\;P_{\pi}\;\mathsf{vec}\big(G\big).

Note that the operators
(U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U)
and
P_{\pi}
commute, since

\displaystyle(U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U)\;P_{\pi}\;(e_{i_{1}}\otimes e_{i_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes e_{i_{s}})

\displaystyle=(U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U)\;\big(e_{i_{\pi^{-1}(1)}}\otimes e_{i_{\pi^{-1}(2)}}\otimes\cdots\otimes e_{i_{\pi^{-1}(s)}}\big)

\displaystyle=\big(Ue_{i_{\pi^{-1}(1)}}\big)\otimes\big(Ue_{i_{\pi^{-1}(2)}}\big)\otimes\cdots\otimes\big(Ue_{i_{\pi^{-1}(s)}}\big)

\displaystyle=P_{\pi}\Big(\big(Ue_{i_{1}}\big)\otimes\big(Ue_{i_{2}}\big)\otimes\cdots\otimes\big(Ue_{i_{s}}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=P_{\pi}\;(U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U)\;(e_{i_{1}}\otimes e_{i_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes e_{i_{s}}).

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle(U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U)\;\mathsf{vec}\big(\zeta\big)=\frac{1}{s!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathfrak{S}_{s}}P_{\pi}\;(U\otimes U\otimes\cdots\otimes U)\;\mathsf{vec}\big(G\big),

as claimed. This concludes the proof. ∎
